Key decisions:\n   - Use `import reprlib` at top of core.py (stdlib, no circular risk)\n   - The format string is: `f\"\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500 {stage} \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500 {' | '.join(f'{k}={reprlib.repr(v)}' for k, v in items.items())}\"`\n   - For wrapping: if the items portion exceeds ~120 chars, split into multiple lines with 4-space indent\n   - debug_anomaly format: `f\"  \u26a1 {stage}: {message}\"`\n\n3. PARALLEL EXECUTION: T3-T21 all depend only on T1 (not on each other). An executor can work through them in any order. T22 (sweep) must wait for all of T3-T21 to avoid double-touching files.\n\n4. JUDGMENT CALLS FOR THE SWEEP (T22): The plan says 809 tagged calls across 75 files. Many of these will already be handled by T3-T21. The sweep catches stragglers. For each remaining tagged call, the executor must decide: is this a chokepoint summary (\u2192debug_block), an anomaly report (\u2192debug_anomaly), or normal-path noise (\u2192delete)? The heuristic: if the log tells you WHERE you are in the pipeline, it's a chokepoint. If it tells you something UNEXPECTED happened, it's an anomaly. If it confirms the EXPECTED happened, delete it.\n\n5. TESTING: The project uses pytest. Run with `python -m pytest tests/` or similar. The new test file (T2) should be self-contained with no external dependencies beyond the logging module itself. For T23, focus on tests that import or mock logging \u2014 those are most likely to have format-dependent assertions.\n\n6. reprlib.repr() DEFAULT TRUNCATION: Default maxstring is 30 chars. This may be too short for paths or prompts.    task_id=task_id,\n+        )\n*** End Patch\n"

The plan's line numbers are from a prior read and may be offset. Always read the current file state before making edits.\n\n2. T1 IS THE KEYSTONE: Everything depends on debug_block/debug_anomaly existing in core.py. Get this right first. Key decisions:\n   - Use `import reprlib` at top of core.py (stdlib, no circular risk)\n   - The format string is: `f\"\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500 {stage} \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500 {' | '.join(f'{k}={reprlib.repr(v)}' for k, v in items.items())}\"`\n   - For wrapping: if the items portion exceeds ~120 chars, split into multiple lines with 4-space indent\n   - debug_anomaly format: `f\"  \u26a1 {stage}: {message}\"`\n\n3. PARALLEL EXECUTION: T3-T21 all depend only on T1 (not on each other). An executor can work through them in any order. T22 (sweep) must wait for all of T3-T21 to avoid double-touching files.\n\n4. JUDGMENT CALLS FOR THE SWEEP (T22): The plan says 809 tagged calls across 75 files. Many of these will already be handled by T3-T21. The sweep catches stragglers. For each remaining tagged call, the executor must decide: is this a chokepoint summary (\u2192debug_block), an anomaly report (\u2192debug_anomaly), or normal-path noise (\u2192delete)? The heuristic: if the log tells you WHERE you are in the pipeline, it's a chokepoint. If it tells you something UNEXPECTED happened, it's an anomaly. If it confirms the EXPECTED happened, delete it.\n\n5. TESTING: The project uses pytest. Run with `python -m pytest tests/` or similar. The new test file (T2) should be self-contained with no external dependencies beyond the logging module itself. For T23, focus on tests that import or mock logging \u2014 those are most likely to have format-dependent assertions.\n\n6. reprlib.repr() DEFAULT TRUNCATION: Default maxstring is 30 chars. This may be too short for paths or prompts. Consider creating a module-level `_repr = reprlib.Repr(); _repr.maxstring = 80` instance for the debug_block method to use, keeping values readable while still bounded.",
